(45) Rubber hose to cylinder head water pipe | California models incorporate Vacuum Delay Valve (30) between Thermal Vacuum Valve (25) and B.P.T. Valve (32); Non-California models connect directly via hose (35). |

| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-1** | `afm[1].gif` | `cutaway_flow` | AFM-1 | Cutaway schematic of Bosch L-Jetronic Air Flow Meter (AFM) showing internal air bypass, damper chamber, flap, helical spring, and potentiometer housing | Measuring flap, Helical return spring, Damper chamber, Damper plate (counterweight vane), Main air passage, By-pass port — (Helical spring) Helical return spring controlling flap resistance; (Case) Cast aluminum main housing; (Damper chamber) Sealed chamber damping intake air pulses; (Damper plate) Vane counteracting intake air pulsations; (Flap) Main airflow measuring flap; (From air cleaner) Air inlet from air filter; (By-pass port) Idle air bypass channel beneath main air passage; (Air by-pass screw) Idle air bypass adjustment screw; (To intake manifold) Air outlet to throttle body and intake manifold; (Air temperature sensor) Intake air temperature thermistor probe; (Potentiometer) Potentiometer resistive board and wiper assembly; (To EFI harness) Electrical connector socket to engine wiring harness | Air-fuel ratio nominal target: 12:1 to 15:1; Air temperature sensor enrichment threshold: ≤ 20°C (70°F) | Bypass channel allows idle airflow to bypass the measuring flap. If the bypass screw is closed completely, backfire ('front fire') shock waves cannot vent through the passage and will bend or destroy the measuring flap. |
| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-2** | `hal1.jpg` | `graph_chart` | AFM-1 | Characteristic curve of Air-Fuel Ratio (AFR) vs. Zirconia Oxygen Sensor output voltage showing steep transition at stoichiometric (14.7:1) | Sensor Output Voltage (Y-axis, 0 - 1.0 V), Air-Fuel Ratio (X-axis, 11:1 - 18:1), Stoichiometric transition cliff (14.7:1), Rich zone (12 - 14.5:1), Lean zone (15 - 18:1) — (Sensor Output Voltage) Y-axis spanning 0.0 to 1.0 Volts; (Air-Fuel Ratio) X-axis spanning 11:1 to 18:1 AFR; (Rich Mixture Region) 12:1 to 14.5:1 AFR corresponds to 0.90 - 0.95 V; (Stoichiometric Transition) Steep voltage cliff at ~14.7:1 AFR; (Lean Mixture Region) 15:1 to 18:1 AFR corresponds to 0.12 - 0.15 V | Nominal rich voltage: ~0.9 V (AFR 12:1 - 14.5:1); Stoichiometric switch: 14.7:1 AFR; Nominal lean voltage: ~0.15 V (AFR 15:1 - 18:1) | Narrow-band O2 sensors exhibit extreme sensitivity right at 14.7:1 AFR but provide little resolution deep in rich or lean zones. Tuning the AFM without a wideband AFR meter relies on spark plug color reading, fingering responsiveness, and fuel economy measurement. |
| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-3** | `afm1.jpg` | `component_location` | AFM-1 | Picture 1: AFM mechanical chamber showing locking screw for black wheel, factory glue blob, reference mark, counterweight, return-to-zero shock absorber, and fuel pump shut-off switch | Black toothed adjustment wheel, Locking screw with clamp tab for black wheel, Coil spring (clock spring), Zinc counterweight, Return to zero shock absorber spring, Fuel pump shut-off switch leaf contacts — (Locking Screw for Black Wheel) Upper left screw securing the toothed black spring tension wheel; (Reference Mark) Red paint / scribe line marking original factory tooth position against casting; (Glue Blob) Original factory silicone / glue seal locking black wheel; broken blob indicates prior tampering; (Return to Zero shock absorber) Curved spring steel strip absorbing rotational inertia when flap snaps shut; (Fuel Pump Shut Off Switch) Dual contact leaf switch actuated by wiper arm to cut fuel pump when engine stalls; (Idle "flow" adjuster) Access port for internal idle bypass needle valve | Return-to-zero shock absorber clearance: contacts ~5 mm (1/2 cm) before physical stop; Pin 36-39 fuel pump switch: Flap closed = infinite Ω (open), Flap cracked = 0 Ω (closed) | WARNING: The black toothed wheel is under spring tension! Loosening the locking screw without holding firm finger pressure on the wheel will cause it to violently recoil ('sprong') and lose all factory calibration. ALWAYS scribe a reference mark on the tooth and case before loosening! |
| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-4** | `afm2.jpg` | `component_location` | AFM-1 | Picture 2: AFM potentiometer board, wiper contacts, second glue blob locking screw, and carbon resistance trace | Ceramic potentiometer circuit board, Dual beryllium copper wiper contacts, Curved carbon resistance trace, Wiper center pivot locking screw, Glue blob #2, Silver nail-like actuator pin for fuel switch — (Potentiometer Carbon Trace) Curved resistive carbon track dividing voltage from ~2.0V to 7.0V; (Wiper Contact) Dual flexible copper wiper fingers sweeping along the carbon track; (Glue Blob) Glue blob #2 securing the center wiper pivot locking screw (adjusts 0V rest position and fuel pump switch arm) | Potentiometer total resistance (Pins 8 & 6): ~180 Ω; Reference resistor (Pins 8 & 9): 100 Ω; Wiper sweep voltage range: ~2.0 V to ~7.0 V | Ensure the wiper does not overshoot the clockwise end of the carbon trace when the flap is closed; it must stop 2-4 mm before the edge. If the wiper shoots off the trace, it registers an open circuit (infinite resistance) and causes severe lean stumble or engine stall. Loosen the center pivot screw to recalibrate wiper rest position. |
| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-5** | `afm3.jpg` | `procedure_step` | AFM-1 | Picture 3: Fingering check technique demonstrating manual deflection of the wiper counterweight towards 'More Fuel' (CCW) and 'Less Fuel' (CW) | Technician finger applying manual deflection, Counterweight and wiper carrier, Direction arrow: More Fuel (CCW), Direction arrow: Less Fuel (CW), Black toothed adjustment wheel, Clock spring — (More Fuel) Counter-clockwise (CCW) rotation opens flap further, increasing output voltage and enriching mixture; (Less Fuel) Clockwise (CW) rotation moves flap towards closed position, lowering output voltage and leaning mixture | Idle wiper displacement: ~10 mm (1 cm) from rest; Stock calibrated AFM optimal fingering deflection: ~4 mm to rich (CCW) for maximum RPM; Test RPM levels: Idle (~750-800 RPM) and steady 3,000 RPM | Always verify ignition timing, mechanical cam timing, valve lash, vacuum leaks, and EFI coolant temperature sensor integrity BEFORE modifying AFM spring tension. False lean symptoms caused by vacuum leaks or over-advanced timing will lead to improper over-enrichment of the AFM. |
| [afm tuning and rebuild](../afm tuning and rebuild.md) | **Fig. AFM-6** | `idleadjust.jpg` | `component_location` | AFM-1 | AFM idle air bypass adjustment screw removed from housing, showing adjustment slot, O-ring seal, and bypass port | Idle air bypass needle screw, Rubber sealing O-ring, Threaded bypass adjustment bore in AFM body, 7-pin electrical harness receptacle, Main air measuring duct (outlet side), Mechanical / electrical upper housing casting — (Yellow circle) Slotted idle air bypass needle adjustment screw and threaded passage bore | Turn Clockwise (CW): Restricts bypass passage -> Forces more air past flap -> Enriches idle mixture; Turn Counter-Clockwise (CCW): Opens bypass passage -> Bypasses more air around flap -> Leans idle mixture | CAUTION: Do not bottom out or completely seal the idle bypass screw. When an intake backfire ('front fire') occurs due to lean mixture, explosive pressure waves vent harmlessly through the bypass passage. If the screw is closed shut, the pressure wave has nowhere to go and will bend or fracture the delicate aluminum measuring flap. |
